#165. Nubank, Latin America's largest digital bank, is turning its eyes toward the US — where foreign rivals have failed

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nubank is the largest digital bank in Latin America — now it's turning its eyes towards the US.

Cristina Junqueira, the co-founder and US CEO of Nubank, sat down with Dan DeFrancesco, executive editor and anchor of Business Insider Today, at Business Insider's The Long Play, to talk about the digital bank's expansion on Thursday.

Since its founding in 2013, the fintech bank has pushed for financial inclusivity in Latin America by providing access to banking and financial services through its mobile app. Headquartered in Junqueira's home country of Brazil, the simple, low-cost, and digital-first approach discarded the need for physical banks, which are at times harder to reach in parts of Latin America — a key part of its success in the region.

It all started with a purple credit card with no fees.

"We had this vision that data, design, technology, completely transforms people's relationship with money," she said.

Junqueira previously worked as an executive at Itaú Unibanco, the largest private-sector financial giant in the region, also headquartered in Brazil and now a direct competitor of Nubank. Today, it is the largest company in Brazil based on primary banking relationships, and two-thirds of the country's adult population are customers, she said.

The digital bank is set to expand into the US after opening a branch in September, operating under the name Nu. This comes after expansion into Mexico and Colombia in 2021.

"We always saw the thesis as a global one," she said. "Everywhere in the world, people want more convenience. People want more transparency. People want simpler experiences managing their finances. People still get like paralyzed by complexity through their financial lives."

Entering America's crowded banking sector is a risk — but for Junqueira, entering no country goes without risk.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

"The US is a very hard market, but I haven't found easy ones either," she said. "Building a bank is hard everywhere, so you might as well go for the biggest payout."

In the US, she said getting a bank account is still "surprisingly hard" and normally requires you to go to a physical branch, while Nu offers an easier alternative to that, she said. Foreign digital-first banks have tried to bring their platforms to the US before and failed. German fintech N26 and UK fintech Monzo both tried to expand their businesses to the US, and ultimately withdrew from the market.

The American consumer is unique, which is why Junqueira aims to take a different approach than her competitors to earn trust and scale the platform. She said most other companies started with debit cards, while Nubank is the first to start with credit cards.

Starting people out with a debit card "requires you to build a lot of trust with those customers because you're asking them to deposit their money with you," she said. "In our case, all the countries that we started, we started with credit. This is a very different relationship because you're the one trusting the consumers," she said.

Some Latin American immigrants who already live in the US are responding positively to Nu as it enters the market.

"I've been positively surprised not only by the amount of people, sure, but Brazilians, Mexicans, and others are here and have been waiting for this," she said.

Junqueira also pointed to emerging AI technology and how it could impact the company and the banking sector. She said the company is "heavily leveraging AI," integrating it in credit underwriting, customer support, and product development.

Brazil's presidential election on Sunday made headlines as President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva unexpectedly won a runoff against Flávio Bolsonaro, who fled the country to the US after he was under investigation for alleged corruption and money laundering. When asked about how she reacted to the big market sell-off in response, Junqueira said volatility is part of operating in a country like Brazil, but having 13 years of experience in the country has helped them learn they are going to run the company for the long run.

"We got to focus on the fundamentals, and the reality is, last quarter we turned over a billion dollars in net income," she said. "Markets don't like surprises, right? But we manage our business regardless of what happens."

The founder said one of the hardest things Nu will have to do in the US is build a brand that connects with users on an "emotional" level. Junqueira's dream scenario, five years from now, is to find evidence of that connection in the US.

One of the ways Nubank is attempting to spread its brand is through a recently signed deal with the Mercedes F1 team. Junqueira pointed to fans' passion for specific teams, as they follow the championship around the world, which was part of why they wanted to take part.

"We got a lot of visibility out of that, and we're working with them on brand activations," she said. "We're producing very interesting activations for consumers that help us amplify the branding fast, and that really help us bring this partnership to life."

Another huge part of the digital bank's brand is gender equality. In 2021, Nubank set a goal for 50% of Nubank's leadership to be women by 2025. Junqueira said today that the company's workforce is over 45% women, with many in leadership positions. In the four countries where they operate, all top leaders are women. Junqueira said the biggest challenge of being an executive while raising a family is not having the flexibility to juggle the demands of both roles.

"The reality is, as hard as we have to work, I think the hardest part is not having enough flexibility," she said. "It has been wonderful for us to have that type of opportunity, for us to really be able to tap into this part of the workforce that often struggles to have this type of career opportunity."

Correction — October 29, 2026: An earlier version of this article misstated Junqueira's title; she is the co-founder and CEO of US Nubank. The article also has been updated to note that the top leaders in the four countries where Nubank operates are women.

#307. Penurunan Beban Bunga Belum Mampu Dongkrak Kinerja Bank Swasta di Kuartal III-2026

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Aura Putri | Editor: Avanty Nurdiana

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. Penurunan beban bunga belum cukup menyelamatkan kinerja seluruh bank swasta pada kuartal III-2026. Memang, ada yang masih mampu mengerek laba, tetapi tekanan pendapatan bunga bersih dan lonjakan biaya pencadangan membuat laba sebagian bank justru merosot. Ambil contoh, PT Bank CIMB Niaga Tbk (BNGA) mencatatkan laba bersih turun 14,75% secara tahunan (yoy) sebesar Rp 4,49 triliun sepanjang Januari-September 2026. Meski beban bunga berhasil turun 6,2% yoy menjadi Rp 7,99 triliun, tapi pendapatan bunga terkontraksi 4% menjadi Rp 17,85 triliun. Alhasil, pendapatan bunga bersih tetap turun 2,2% menjadi Rp 9,86 triliun. Beban provisi CIMB Niaga juga tercatat melonjak 65,2% menjadi Rp 2,43 triliun.

Baca Juga: Transaksi wondr by BNI Tembus Rp 1.774 Triliun, Naik 25% hingga September 2026 Meski begitu, total kredit tumbuh 6,7% yoy menjadi Rp 242,6 triliun per September 2026. Pertumbuhan kredit ini didorong oleh segmen korporasi yang tumbuh 15,1% yoy menjadi Rp 102.07 triliun. Adapun dana murah atau current account and savings account (CASA) meningkat 4,4% yoy menjadi Rp 197 triliun. Presiden Direktur CIMB Niaga Lani Darmawan bilang, tekanan margin bunga dan kenaikan pencadangan menjadi tantangan bagi kinerja perusahaan. Sehingga kedepannya, bank akan lebih selektif menyalurkan kredit dan mengoptimalkan pendanaan. “Fokus kami memperkuat kualitas aset, mengoptimalkan komposisi pendanaan, serta meningkatkan pendapatan berbasis komisi dengan tetap menjaga efisiensi biaya,” ujar Lani dalam keterangan resmi, Kamis (29/10/2026). Senada, PT Bank Permata Tbk (BNLI) juga mencatatkan laba bersih turun 10,9% yoy menjadi sebesar Rp 2,57 triliun. Pendapatan bunga Permata Bank turun 5,67% yoy menjadi Rp 12,12 triliun. Meski beban bunga juga turun 10,23% yoy menjadi Rp 4,73 triliun, pendapatan bunga bersih tetap terkoreksi 2,49% yoy menjadi Rp 7,38 triliun. Meski demikian, Permata Bank mencatatkan pertumbuhan kredit sebesar 10,1% yoy menjadi Rp 172,9 triliun hingga kuartal III-2026. Pertumbuhan tersebut ditopang oleh penyaluran kredit konvensional yang meningkat 13,9% yoy menjadi Rp 153,4 triliun.

Baca Juga: Cermati Strategi Pencadangan Perbankan di Kuartal III-2026 Adapun CASA turun sekitar 9% yoy Rp 107,86 triliun. Namun memang, CASA masih mendominasi sebesar 56,4% terhadap total Dana Pihak Ketiga (DPK). Direktur Utama Permata Bank Meliza M. Rusli mengatakan, bankakan terus fokus memperkuat CASA melalui penguatan transaksi nasabah serta optimalisasi di seluruh kanal. Sebaliknya, PT Bank Danamon Indonesia Tbk (BDMN) berhasil membukukan laba bersih sebesar Rp 3,49 triliun hingga September 2026, naik 18,5% yoy. Pendapatan bunga bersih Danamon juga tumbuh 8,9% yoy menjadi Rp 16,22 triliun. Pendapatan bunga naik 7,9% menjadi Rp 22,35 triliun, meski beban bunga juga naik 5,3% menjadi Rp 6,13 triliun. Di sisi pendanaan, CASA Bank Danamon tumbuh 7% yoy menjadi Rp 73,9 triliun per September 2026. Direktur Utama Danamon Nobuya Kawasaki mengatakan, pertumbuhan intermediasi dan pendanaan tetap terjaga dengan didukung pengelolaan risiko dan kualitas aset. "Kami akan tetap berfokus pada pertumbuhan yang sehat, kualitas aset yang terjaga, serta penciptaan nilai jangka panjang bagi seluruh pemangku kepentingan," ujarnya dalam keterangan resmi, Kamis (29/10/2026). Sementara itu, PT Bank Central Asia Tbk (BBCA) juga mencatat laba bersih naik 4,2% yoy menjadi Rp 45,21 triliun pada kuartal III-2026. Ini sejalan dengan pendapatan bunga bersih yang naik 1,2% menjadi Rp 64,9 triliun. Adapun beban provisi BCA juga tercatat menurun 7,5% menjadi Rp 3,6 triliun.

Baca Juga: Laba Bank Danamon Tumbuh 19% Jadi Rp 3,5 Triliun hingga September 2026 Dari sisi kredit, BCA juga berhasil membukukan penyaluran kredit mencapai Rp 1.063 triliun, tumbuh 12,6% yoy. Penyaluran kredit BCA turut ditopang kredit korporasi yang tumbuh 20,9% yoy menjadi Rp 528,1 triliun. Direktur Keuangan BCA Vera Eve Lim mengatakan, pertumbuhan laba BCA ditopang ekspansi kredit dan pertumbuhan dana murah. Adapun CASA BCA tumbuh 10,4% yoy. Rinciannya, giro meningkat 14,9% yoy, sedangkan tabungan tumbuh 7,4% yoy. PT Bank Panin Indonesia Tbk (PNBN) mencatat kenaikan laba bersih sebesar 1,82% menajadi Rp 2,15 triliun hingga sembilan bulan tahun ini. Pendapatan bunga PaninBank tercatat turun tipis 0,34% yoy menjadi sebesar Rp 11,63 triliun. Beban bunga berhasil turun 5,85% yoy menjadi Rp 4,81 triliun. Alhasil, pendapatan bunga bersih PaninBank meningkat 3,95% yoy menjadi Rp 6,82 triliun. Adapun penyaluran kredit PaninBank tumbuh terbatas sebesar 0,4% mencapai Rp 142,36 triliun per September 2026, Presiden Direktur PaninBank Herwidayatmo mengatakan, kenaikan laba bank turut didorong oleh turunnya beban bunga. Adapun hingga akhir tahun bank akan terus mengupayakan penurunan biaya dana. "PaninBank terus berupaya menurunkan biaya dana dengan memperbaiki komposisi dana pihak ketiga," ujar Herwidayatmo dalam keterangan tertulis, Rabu (28/10/2026).

#316. Transaksi wondr by BNI Tembus Rp 1.774 Triliun, Naik 25% hingga September 2026

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Selvi Mayasari | Editor: Ignatia Maria Sri Sayekti

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. PT Bank Negara Indonesia Tbk (BBNI) mencatatkan nilai transaksi melalui aplikasi wondr by BNI mencapai Rp 1.774 triliun hingga September 2026. Nilai tersebut tumbuh 25% secara tahunan atau year on year (YoY).

Pertumbuhan transaksi tersebut sejalan dengan peningkatan jumlah pengguna wondr by BNI yang mencapai 16,6 juta hingga September 2026, melonjak 58% YoY.

Corporate Secretary BNI, Okki Rushartomo mengatakan, peningkatan aktivitas transaksi digital mencerminkan perubahan kebutuhan dan kebiasaan nasabah dalam mengakses layanan perbankan.

"Bagi BNI, menjaga kepercayaan nasabah dimulai dari bagaimana kami memberikan layanan dalam keseharian. Kami terus mengembangkan layanan digital sekaligus meningkatkan kualitas pelayanan di kantor cabang agar nasabah semakin mudah memenuhi kebutuhan perbankannya," ujar Okki dalam keterangan tertulis, Kamis (29/10/2026).

Tak hanya di segmen ritel, pertumbuhan transaksi digital juga terjadi pada layanan perbankan bisnis dan korporasi melalui BNIdirect.

Baca Juga: Pengguna wondr by BNI Tembus 15,1 Juta Hingga Akhir Juni 2026

Hingga kuartal III-2026, nilai transaksi BNIdirect mencapai Rp 9.324 triliun, meningkat 16% YoY. Sementara itu, jumlah penggunanya tumbuh 19% YoY menjadi 293.000 pengguna.

BNI juga memperluas layanan digital melalui wondrZ yang menyasar anak dan remaja, serta wondr Merchant untuk membantu pelaku usaha menerima pembayaran digital dan mengelola transaksi bisnis.

Peningkatan aktivitas transaksi digital tersebut turut mendukung pertumbuhan dana murah atau current account saving account (CASA) BNI.

Hingga September 2026, CASA BNI mencapai Rp 733,7 triliun, tumbuh 19,6% YoY. Pertumbuhan tersebut melampaui pertumbuhan CASA industri perbankan yang tercatat sebesar 9,7%.

Penguatan dana murah tersebut turut mendukung kapasitas intermediasi perseroan. Hingga kuartal III-2026, penyaluran kredit BNI tumbuh 22,2% YoY.

Menurut Okki, pengembangan layanan digital tidak hanya ditujukan untuk memberikan kemudahan transaksi bagi nasabah, tetapi juga menjadi bagian dari strategi mendukung pertumbuhan bisnis perseroan.

Baca Juga: Transaksi Digital BNI Capai Rp 6.997 Triliun pada Semester I-2026

"Penguatan layanan digital tersebut berjalan beriringan dengan peningkatan kualitas layanan secara menyeluruh. BNI terus membangun pengalaman nasabah yang konsisten melalui penguatan layanan di sisi depan yang ditopang keandalan operasional, sistem, dan infrastruktur di back end," jelas Okki.

Selain memperkuat layanan digital, BNI juga mengembangkan pemanfaatan teknologi untuk mendukung layanan sektor publik. Upaya tersebut memperoleh tiga penghargaan GovMedia Awards 2026 di Singapura untuk kategori Digital Initiative, Process Optimization, dan Public Sector Initiative.

BNI juga meraih posisi Top 3 sektor Financial Services dan Top 4 BUMN dengan Layanan Terbaik dalam Danantara Indonesia CX100 Award 2026 yang digelar pada 13 Oktober 2026.

Di sisi tata kelola, BNI memperoleh penghargaan Leadership in Corporate Governance pada The 17th IICD Corporate Governance Award 2026 serta masuk dalam daftar Top 50 Emiten BigCap Public Listed Company.

Ke depan, BNI menyatakan akan terus memperkuat kapabilitas digital, keandalan operasional, dan kualitas pelayanan di kantor cabang guna mendukung kebutuhan transaksi nasabah serta pertumbuhan bisnis secara berkelanjutan.

#174. Barry Diller shares why his deal to buy MGM fell apart

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Media mogul Barry Diller thinks businesses that operate in the real world have a strong protective moat against AI.

That was the inspiration for his company People Inc.'s June bid for MGM Resorts International's casino and hotel business, which he called "forever assets."

As some consumers turn to AI chatbots to complete tasks like buying plane tickets or managing subscriptions, digital-only businesses could get boxed out. That creates real risks for internet brands like Expedia Inc., where Diller is an investor and board member. It's less of a problem for companies whose businesses exist in the real world, he said.

Nothing is going to replace Las Vegas, Diller said — and no one's going to buy another one. Gaming is only part of the equation, he said.

"No disintermediation is coming between a person who wants to go to a place, and that place," he said, speaking to BI's Chief Correspondent Peter Kafka at Business Insider's The Long Play event in New York on Thursday.

Follow along with our coverage from Business Insider's The Long Play

People Inc. first took a 27% stake in MGM in 2020. The company, which operates magazines such as Food & Wine, People, and Southern Living, later offered to buy the Las Vegas brand outright in June but withdrew its bid in September.

Diller said the deal fell apart after he became uncomfortable with the proposed structure, which involved taking on debt and asking third parties for money.

MGM later offered to buy People Inc. instead, but told investors on Wednesday that it had shelved the plan. Diller said it was "highly unlikely" that MGM was interested in People's magazine assets, but rather it had hoped to buy back People's MGM shares.

People remains MGM's largest investor, though it has agreed not to buy more than a 29.9% stake in the company and to vote with MGM's board in connection with a change-of-control transaction.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

People, which, beyond its magazine portfolio, also owns stakes in The Daily Beast and the car-rental company Turo, laid off 195 workers on Thursday, per a memo from CEO Neil Vogel, which Business Insider viewed. The vast majority of impacted staffers worked on its "print and legacy web businesses," the memo said. The company has "added 320 new employees so far in 2026," it said.

Diller told Kafka that the layoffs were a sign of a healthy company that has a profitable print business in a "steady kind of decline."

"We've been transferring out people in these legacy businesses and transferring in people to do the new work," he said.

Diller is a longtime media operator. He served as chairman and CEO of Paramount Pictures in the 1970s and 80s, before joining Fox Inc. and helping to start Fox Broadcasting with Rupert Murdoch in the mid-1980s. He then worked at QVC before serving as CEO of IAC and taking on roles at Expedia Group. He is on the board of directors at MGM.

#184. Instagram is trying to woo YouTube creators with six-figure checks

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Instagram wants what YouTube has — and it's willing to pay up to get it.

In an effort to persuade content creators to post long-form video on Instagram, the company is paying a select number of creators hefty sums, according to people familiar with the deals.

Not every deal is equal. The Meta-owned platform has offered select creators $50,000 to post long-form video to its new "Features" product, while others have been offered six-figure deals, some of which have reached $500,000, Business Insider exclusively learned.

"We've been public about the fact that we are funding Features content directly with some creators right now to support them," a Meta spokesperson told Business Insider. "The details of those deals are confidential, but we're excited to see the content going live."

On Monday, Instagram unveiled an overhaul of its video formats. Most importantly, it's resurrecting IGTV — a long-form video product Instagram launched in 2018 and sunset in 2022 — in the form of Features. The horizontal, wide-screen videos will exist within the Instagram app, and can be as long as a feature film: 90 minutes.

Long-form video isn't exactly what people are used to watching on Instagram. Reels, the platform's answer to TikTok's meteoric rise, has redefined the Instagram experience and is driving time-spent and revenue across Meta's apps.

YouTube, however, reigns king as the internet's destination for sit-down, longer-form videos.

Instagram knows this. The deals do not require exclusivity to Instagram, multiple people familiar with the agreements said; one person familiar with the offers said it's asking creators to post to Instagram any of the long-form content they publish to YouTube that same day.

Reza Izad, partner at Underscore Talent, which reps clients like "Subway Takes'" Kareem Rahma and Noah Beck, called the deals an "exciting opportunity" for creators.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

"Every time there's a new feature in these apps that the company's promoting, people will have more opportunity to be seen because there's going to be algorithmic preference to this kind of content," he said. "And unlike short-form, there's not a lot of abundance."

This isn't the first time Instagram has inked paid deals with creators to lure them to post more on Meta's apps.

Last year, Meta was paying creators thousands of dollars to post short-form videos to Instagram and Facebook in the midst of TikTok's potential ban. The company has tested several bonus programs paying creators for the performance of their Reels on Instagram, though those programs have dwindled.

Back in 2021, when IGTV was still part of Instagram's big video bet, it inked deals with creators, funding long-form episodic shows from creators like TikTok's "Sway Boys" and "Dance Moms" alum Nia Sioux. Briefly, IGTV had its own monetization program, too, which let some creators earn a cut from ad revenue on the format.

While Instagram is paying some creators directly, a big question looms over the creator economy: What about the rest of them?

A major draw of YouTube is its long-standing Partner Program, which pays eligible creators a share of the ad revenue generated by their videos. Even though YouTube has increased its eligibility requirements, the potential to turn a hobby or passion project into a cash-earning career has kept YouTube on top as the go-to video platform for creators.

It's no surprise, then, that when Instagram announced its video overhaul, creators immediately had questions about monetization.

"We understand the economics matter a ton, and are exploring multiple options to help long-form creators get paid," Instagram's top executive, Adam Mosseri, wrote in an X post on Wednesday.

#202. Early Anthropic member recalls burnout and big ambitions in the AI lab's early days

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic's executives raced to build a safe AI — and one former employee said that pursuit left him feeling burned out and disillusioned.

In a lengthy Substack post, Ivan Vendrov, who worked as a member of the AI lab's technical staff from late 2022 to mid-2023, described an intense workplace, where fears of AI catastrophe clashed with the urge to build safe AGI for commercial use.

Vendrov began the post by saying his account should not be taken as gospel. He said he recalled the events "from memory," wrote the "gist of the dialogue rather than the exact words used," and that the post is "merely the experience of one guy in a junior role during a short window of time."

Still, it's a remarkable look into the early days of one of the world's preeminent AI companies, with a named employee explaining how the company's formative period created a highly particular workplace.

It also comes days after Anthropic filed to go public. Anthropic declined to comment on this story.

Vendrov, whose LinkedIn says he now works at Midjourney, wrote that he was weighing contract offers from OpenAI and Anthropic in 2022.

He says he decided to join Anthropic after lunch with one of the AI company's cofounders, who said they were already chatting with regulators in Washington.

A recruiter asked whether he wanted to pledge two-thirds of his equity to charity.

"All the co-founders have made the pledge," Vendrov recalled the recruiter saying. He wrote that he was unsure whether the job offer was contingent on his agreement, so he said yes.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

Anthropic's S-1 filing describes an employee donation-matching program, where workers can pledge a "meaningful portion" of their equity awards. The company reported that 50% of its employees are included in the program.

Vendrov described a cramped, ugly office. He said it was stocked with fresh croissants and, later, a popular masseuse came twice a week. He said he was never able to schedule a massage because it was always fully booked.

There was a visit from the FBI once, he wrote. The agent warned that Anthropic was "probably already the target of multiple foreign intelligence agencies."

Anthropic also hosted a sushi chef in the office, Vendrov wrote — but the luxe experience was met with resistance. The following day, he said a cofounder wrote in the company Slack that it set the wrong tone.

"We're not here to get rich and eat caviar," they said, per Vendrov's memory, before pitching future dinners of rice and beans.

Vendrov's post detailed sleepless nights, walks with other stressed-out colleagues, and moments where he felt he couldn't be honest about his emotions with colleagues.

Eight months after signing his contract offer, Vendrov said he wrote a resignation letter. As he drafted the letter, he said he thought about describing his doubts to the company, but worried it would create doubt in colleagues who were "smarter than me, more competent than me, more solidly well-intentioned than me."

Instead, he said he praised Anthropic as an "unbelievably good organization."

Vendrov did not respond to a request for comment from Business Insider.

#209. Big Oil earnings are set to surge. Why investors may want to be cautious

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

That's not a reference to the reportedly amazing new documentary about disgraced Theranos founder Elizabeth Holmes. It's about the next few days and next week: days that will be dominated by earnings, a massive conference, and more on Europe's energy woes.

I'll hit more on energy earnings in The Actionable Insider below.

Next week for me, it's multiple flights through multiple time zones. First up is one of the most important energy conferences in the world: ADIPEC in Abu Dhabi. It's a who's who of the energy world. I'll be speaking with some of the most influential energy executives in the world. You'll see some clips on CNBC about it and then I'll write about it here next week. I'll also be meeting privately with some of the biggest power players in energy and having great conversations with country and company leaders about oil, gas, commodities, Hormuz, and more - all ahead of what some fear may be another round of hits on Iran or Iranian energy infrastructure. It remains a tense time.

That will be followed later in the week by a deeper dive into Europe's slow-rolling energy crisis. The Iran war and Hormuz risk have added another layer to an already risky situation, but let's be clear: it is not the reason why Europe is in an energy bind. Russia's horrible invasion and now four-year war on Ukraine added gasoline to a fire that began burning with bad policy decisions years ago. I have been covering the European energy story for five years - from the UK, Germany, Belgium, and the Netherlands - and next week I'll be in Italy highlighting where Europe is right now. Hint: it's not good. Natural gas storage levels are below their recent average, and prices are near record highs for imported LNG.

Of course, there is a risk that travel plans change, much as they did a few weeks ago around a planned trip to Riyadh. That was scuttled after the Houthis launched cowardly and deadly attacks on civilian targets in Riyadh and other Saudi Arabian cities.

Speaking of the Middle East, on my X account I posted a rather blurry picture sent by a friend of mine in Dubai a few days ago. On a morning swim, he spotted a U.S. Navy ship standing guard in the distance in the Arabian (Persian) Gulf. It's blurry because he had to zoom in from a distance, but regardless, I think it tells a thousand words. Oil is flowing through Hormuz in part because the great U.S. Navy is making sure ships get through. This ship and the brave men and women on it are part of that. But there's also the risk that the Iran war heats back up, with new U.S. assaults on Iranian targets. If that happens, oil is likely to pop. We live in a day-to-day, or even hour-by-hour, world.

Keep calm, carry on… and keep a warship at the ready.

You're going to see everything… including earnings and guidance.

The next few days are critical for energy investors. Earnings roll out from ExxonMobil (XOM), Chevron (CVX), Marathon Petroleum (MPC), and ConocoPhillips (COP).

I'll just be blunt: these numbers are going to be big. Texas-sized big.

The expected numbers for ExxonMobil, Chevron, and ConocoPhillips are above. All three are expected to more than double of EPS from last year. Then there are the refining-heavy companies. Those are a different thing altogether.

Marathon Petroleum's earnings estimate is $25.37 a share. That's not a typo. Three months ago, EPS came in at $17.73, versus an estimate of "only" $14.27. And consider this: just one year ago, the Ohio-based company earned $4.51 per share. The jump in oil prices and - more importantly - the huge "crack spread" on refined products have sales expected to increase by $13 billion in just one year.

For all four, guidance may be more important than the earnings themselves. What these companies say about the future and capital spending matters most. Any changes to spending plans will be closely watched by investors.

These numbers are also likely to be closely watched by the political class. They are likely to use these big numbers to excoriate the industry as oil prices rise. An industry source tells me that the industry is "used to" the political attacks, but wishes there were more focus on the boom-or-bust nature of much of the industry, and that the money these firms earn now will help brace against the bad times and when - not if - oil drops again at some point in the future.

Whatever your bent is on Big Oil, get the popcorn ready, because the next week will be big for all you energy investors - in many ways - and hopefully you'll see everything.

For investors, caution is warranted on the energy space, says BTIG's chart analyst Jonathan Krinksy. On refiners specifically, he notes that the S&P Refining Index is up nearly 160% in 2026, its best year in at least three decades by far.

"At a minimum we should expect consolidation, but more likely some significant downside reversion," wrote Krinsky earlier this week.

Krinsky doesn't like the look of the charts on Valero and Marathon Petroleum. On the latter, the analyst said the stock "hasn't quite broken its uptrend, but feels like it's coming."

This week, we spoke with U.S. Energy Secretary Chris Wright. He joined us from a nuclear power plant in rural Illinois. His office is offering billions of dollars in loan guarantees to help build out more nuclear power capacity in the U.S. We spoke with the secretary about that, as well as oil, Iran, Hormuz, and the importance of U.S. LNG in keeping Europe running right now.

Shell nearly doubles quarterly profit year-on-year as refining margins soar to record high

Sinopec's third-quarter profit jumps 57% on higher oil prices, refining margins (Reuters)

Trump methane policy rollback may have minimal impact on crude prices, EPA says

This German Energy Giant Is an Overlooked AI Bargain (WSJ)

#332. Cinema XXI (CNMA) Tambah 14 Layar Bioskop Sepanjang 2026

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Leni Wandira | Editor: Noverius Laoli

KONTAN.CO.ID – JAKARTA. PT Nusantara Sejahtera Raya Tbk (CNMA), pengelola jaringan bioskop Cinema XXI, menambah 14 layar bioskop sepanjang 2026.

Ekspansi tersebut dilakukan melalui pembukaan empat lokasi baru hingga 29 Oktober 2026, seiring upaya perseroan memperluas jaringan secara selektif ke wilayah potensial.

Keempat lokasi baru tersebut yakni Ciplaz Cipanas XXI, 23 Semarang XXI, Grand Mall Maros XXI, dan Sorong Modern City XXI.

Dengan penambahan tersebut, Cinema XXI kini mengoperasikan 271 lokasi bioskop dengan total 1.402 layar yang tersebar di 55 kota dan 31 kabupaten di Indonesia hingga 29 Oktober 2026.

Baca Juga: Cinema XXI (CNMA) Optimistis Bisnis Bioskop Makin Solid, Ini Strateginya

Direktur Utama Cinema XXI Suryo Suherman mengatakan, perseroan terus memperkuat bisnis inti sekaligus mengembangkan potensi setiap lini usaha untuk menopang pertumbuhan jangka panjang.

“Melalui peningkatan kualitas layanan, inovasi produk, dan optimalisasi jaringan bioskop, kami berupaya menghadirkan pengalaman yang relevan bagi pelanggan sekaligus memperluas peluang bisnis perseroan,” ujar Suryo dalam keterangan tertulis, Kamis (29/10).

Di tengah ekspansi jaringan, CNMA membukukan pendapatan Rp 4,2 triliun hingga kuartal III-2026, dengan laba bersih sebesar Rp 416,3 miliar. Perseroan juga mencatatkan EBITDA Rp 1,2 triliun dan arus kas bersih dari aktivitas operasi Rp 931,4 miliar.

Penjualan tiket bioskop menjadi kontributor terbesar pendapatan dengan nilai Rp 2,5 triliun atau sekitar 59,1% dari total pendapatan.

Sementara itu, segmen makanan dan minuman atau food and beverages(F&B) menyumbang Rp 1,4 triliun, setara 34,7% dari total pendapatan.

Baca Juga: Cinema XXI (CNMA) Buka Bioskop Pertama di Kabupaten Maros

Pada kuartal III-2026, pendapatan CNMA tumbuh 7,5% secara tahunan (year-on-year/YoY) menjadi Rp 1,5 triliun. Pertumbuhan tersebut terutama ditopang kenaikan pendapatan F&B sebesar 13,9% YoY, sejalan dengan peningkatan rata-rata belanja F&B per penonton sebesar 15,8%.

Selain mengembangkan jaringan bioskop, CNMA memperluas sumber pendapatan dari iklan dan platform digital. Pada kuartal III-2026, pendapatan lainnya meningkat 59,1% YoY dengan kontribusi terhadap total pendapatan naik menjadi 6,5%, dibandingkan 4,4% pada periode yang sama tahun lalu.

Perusahaan juga berencana membagikan dividen interim sebesar Rp 4 per saham dengan total nilai Rp 333,1 miliar. Ke depan, CNMA akan melanjutkan ekspansi jaringan secara selektif dan terukur, sembari mengoptimalkan pendapatan serta menjaga efisiensi operasional.

#278. The South West reacts to Budget announcements

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Businesses in Devon and Cornwall said there were both positives and negatives to the budget

Tourism and hospitality leaders have been reacting to this week's Budget.

Among the headlines in chancellor John Healey's first Budget on Wednesday were a Â£100 payment to help millions of households with energy bills, a VAT cut on electricity bills and a freeze on fuel duty, both extended to July.

It also announced Devon would get an elected mayor in 2028 white Cornwall was included as the only area on a pathway to become a Foundation Strategic Authority.

Sue Jewell, chair of South East Cornwall Tourism Association and a holiday accommodation provider, said the budget "gave with one hand and took with the other".

Sue Jewell said the rising minimum wage may lead to some job losses

As part of the Budget legal minimum wage is to rise by 3.9% in April

Jewell said: "I think it's tricky because actually I would believe that quite a lot of people in our business anyway pay over the minimum wage just to retain staff.

"However because of the cost we've cut back on staff.

"Which is sad really because it's local people who could be having local jobs."

Dr Harry Pitts, senior lecturer in Politics at the University of Exeter's Cornwall Campus, said the Â£100 energy payment was one of the "small things to make life a little easier for some households in the immediate period".

Anyone living in a home in council tax bands A to D in England, will receive a one-off Â£100 "Household Energy Payment", funded by extra tax on banks.

Toby Parkins said there was a lot of nervousness ahead of the budget

The Budget announced the threshold at which high street shops start paying business rates in England was to be increased from Â£12,000 to Â£18,000, meaning businesses would pay no rates on properties with a rateable value below this level.

Toby Parkins, interim ceo at the Cornwall Chamber of Commerce, said he predicted this would benefit about 900 extra businesses in Cornwall.

"For the small business that is a big win," he said.

Helen Wylde-Archibald, from the Devon Chamber of Commerce, said she "welcomed some stability".

"The last two have been a bit scary for business," she said, referring to previous Budgets.

She added: "It doesn't necessarily give us all the conditions we want for full growth and strong growth, but the main thing was there was no broad base business tax rises and fuel duty remains frozen until July."

She said she didn't think there was enough in the way of announcements of more support for farming and food, and this had "an impact on the rural economy".

Some farmers said they had not seen much in the Budget that would benefit them

James Kittow, from Butcher & Grazier in Cornwall, said: "It just seems like we've been forgotten again.

"We're an industry that is ready, willing and able to help the country produce the food for the nation."

He added there was no change on inheritance tax for farmers. Since April, inherited farming assets worth more than Â£2.5m face 20% inheritance tax

"I'm looking to plan for a succession... we want to keep farming, we want to keep doing what we love doing but if we're going to get hit on the other end of it then it does make you think twice about what is all this for, and it's just so sad for the future farmers that want to come into the trade," he said.

Andrea Drew, managing director at Lander Mortgage Services, said the key announcement relating to housing was a scheme for first time buyers, and a a "First Time Buyer Isa".

This is an account in which savers can build up a deposit, with the government topping up contributions of up to Â£4,000 each year with a 25% bonus.

"The only negative I would say... on new builds, the housing market isn't just all about new builds, it's about existing property... because not everybody wants to have a new build property," she said.

She added: "The other thing was about the increase in tax for landlords so, they're increasing the tax by 4%."

She said she'd seen landlords who decided to sell their property because of the amount of tax but tenants couldn't afford to buy the homes.

"What tenants need is more homes to choose from, not fewer, and they can't solve the housing shortage by destroying people who are providing the housing," she said.

#45. Why Expertise Alone Isn’t Enough to Earn You Media Coverage

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

Many experts and thought leaders want media coverage but lead with their title and credentials. Editors are looking for something more.

Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.

I hear it all the time. “I’m a doctor and want PR. But I want PR only about doing surgery, because I am a surgeon.”

Alas, that’s not exactly how media relations works.

I know that can sting, and I get it. After all, most experts I talk to have so many opportunities to get featured in the media. Reporters, podcast hosts and editors are looking for credible voices all the time, and an expert with years of hands-on experience is exactly who they want.

So why do so many experts send pitch after pitch and get little or nothing back?

As a longtime publicist and the host of the Grow Your Wellth podcast, I see this from both sides. I receive guest pitches regularly, and they often open with the expert’s credentials: where they trained, what they are certified in, how long they have been in practice. What they less often pitch me is what the episode would be about or what problem my listeners would walk away solving. And I’m left trying to figure out where to place them — or worse — just unable to respond.

Why? Because even when it’s impressive, a resume alone does not make for good programming.

Many experts lead with the job they do every day, because that’s where their credibility lies. But if you want to see more recognition as an expert, that comes from building your visibility around a problem you solve or a unique perspective you bring, not just around your title. I want to show you what that looks like and why it works.

Think about how an editor decides what to cover. They are not asking what you do. They are asking what their reader is struggling with or wants and who can help explain it.

Your daily work is a solution. The problem behind it is the story. Editors write about problems and how to solve them, and they do it more now than ever because readers arrive through search. People type what they are feeling or worried about, and the coverage that earns their attention is the coverage that meets them there.

The same is true when someone asks an AI tool for help. According to OpenAI, more than 300 million questions are asked in ChatGPT every week about health and wellness. Users describe a problem, and the experts who have built their presence around that problem are the ones that surface.

So, if you are getting into PR just to talk about yourself, you are missing a huge visibility opportunity.

Believe it or not, there are nearly a dozen types of media coverage. They include dedicated features, expert commentary, trend-driven coverage and podcasts, among others. Almost everyone wants the dedicated feature, which is the story built around you. But there are far fewer of those opportunities to begin with.

The other types are not consolation prizes. They are how you build the authority that makes a feature possible in the first place.

L&P Aesthetics in Palo Alto is led by two dual board-certified facial plastic surgeons. Like most physicians we work with at FACTEUR PR, they are most excited about discussing what they do each day, which is facial plastic surgery. It is technical, it is fascinating, and they are very good at it.

However, if you ask them or their patients, you’ll find that the problem they solve is bigger than any one procedure; it’s about helping renew their patients’ sense of identity.

That journey happens well before the patient is on the operating table. It may begin with the moment when they look in the mirror and do not quite recognize the person looking back. Maybe it starts with their skin, their eyes, their smile or their hair. In other words, their patients don’t just seek a snatched jawline — they want to feel like themselves again (maybe even better!).

That is the problem these doctors solve for.

To keep that focus, we mapped their authority across five tiers, moving from the broadest conversation to the most personal one. Most media stories in health, wellness and aesthetics happen in the first three layers because that is most searchable and what readers want.

The first layer starts with problem awareness — it’s the universal conversation about why skin, hair and the face age, and what science actually supports. From there, the layers get deeper, going from problem aware to solution aware, and finally landing at the company as the go-to solution.

This approach has earned L&P Aesthetics expert commentary published in Vogue and ELLE, and yes, dedicated features in People, The Daily Beast, USA Today … and counting. The mix matters. Authority is not built with one medium. It is generated across them.

You do not need a new brand to start. Try finishing this sentence: I help [this type of person] who are struggling with [this type of problem] so they can [achieve, feel, or do something they couldn’t before]. If you fill in the blanks with your title or your service, go back and name the feeling or the problem instead.

Then ask yourself three questions. What conversation is already happening around this problem? Where do I have a perspective most people covering it do not? And what are the five topics an editor in my field could cover this month where I could truly help their reader?

Remember, your credentials still matter. They are the proof behind your point of view! But they are not the headline. Build around the problem, join the conversation first, and the coverage you expected from the beginning becomes far more likely to show up and be sought after.

I hear it all the time. “I’m a doctor and want PR. But I want PR only about doing surgery, because I am a surgeon.”

Alas, that’s not exactly how media relations works.

I know that can sting, and I get it. After all, most experts I talk to have so many opportunities to get featured in the media. Reporters, podcast hosts and editors are looking for credible voices all the time, and an expert with years of hands-on experience is exactly who they want.

We'll be in your inbox every morning Monday-Saturday with all the day’s top business news, inspiring stories, best advice and exclusive reporting from Entrepreneur.

#342. Laba Bersih Bank Danamon Tumbuh 19% Jadi Rp 3,5 Triliun, Ini Pendorongnya

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. Bank Danamon Indonesia Tbk (BDMN) membukukan laba bersih konsolidasian sebesar Rp 3,5 triliun, tumbuh 19% secara tahunan (yoy).

Kinerja ini didukung oleh pertumbuhan total kredit dan trade finance konsolidasian sebesar 13% yoy serta peningkatan simpanan nasabah sebesar 12% yoy.

Direktur Utama Danamon Nobuya Kawasaki mengatakan, pertumbuhan intermediasi dan pendanaan tetap terjaga dengan didukung pengelolaan risiko dan kualitas aset yang disiplin.

"Kami akan tetap berfokus pada pertumbuhan yang sehat, kualitas aset yang terjaga, serta penciptaan nilai jangka panjang bagi seluruh pemangku kepentingan," ujarnya dalam keterangan resmi, Kamis (29/10/2026).

Dalam sisi intermediasi, Danamon membukukan total kredit dan trade finance konsolidasian sebesar Rp 232,6 triliun, tumbuh 13% yoy per 30 September 2026.

Baca Juga: Survei HSBC: 84% Manajer Keuangan Indonesia Optimistis AI Dongkrak Treasury

Pertumbuhan ini ditopang oleh kinerja berbagai lini bisnis, termasuk Enterprise Banking and Financial Institution yang meningkat sebesar 20%, SME Banking yang meningkat sebesar 9%, Consumer Banking yang meningkat sebesar 5%, serta anak perusahaan Danamon yang juga mencatatkan pertumbuhan sebesar 7%.

Pada sisi penghimpunan dana, Danamon membukukan total rekening giro, rekening tabungan (current account and savings account atau CASA) dan deposito meningkat 12% yoy menjadi Rp 190,5 triliun.

Sementara pada aspek profitabilitas, pendapatan operasional meningkat 5% yoy menjadi Rp 19,0 triliun. Penurunan biaya kredit sebesar 5% yoy turut mendukung pencapaian laba bersih setelah pajak (NPAT) sebesar Rp 3,5 triliun.

Sejalan dengan hal tersebut, Danamon membukukan margin bunga bersih (net interest margin atau NIM) sebesar 8,0%.

Pada kualitas aset per 30 September 2026, rasio Loan at Risk (LAR) tercatat membaik 190 basis poin menjadi 7,7%, dibandingkan periode yang sama tahun sebelumnya.

Di saat yang sama, rasio cakupan NPL (NPL coverage ratio) mencapai 282,0%.

"Meskipun kondisi pasar tetap kompetitif dan dinamika ekonomi global masih memerlukan perhatian, Danamon terus berfokus pada pengelolaan risiko yang disiplin, kualitas aset yang sehat, serta pertumbuhan bisnis yang berkelanjutan," pungkasnya.

#347. Survei HSBC: 84% Manajer Keuangan Indonesia Optimistis AI Dongkrak Treasury

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Selvi Mayasari | Editor: Avanty Nurdiana

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. Sebanyak 84% manajer keuangan korporasi di Indonesia memperkirakan kecerdasan buatan atau artificial intelligence (AI) akan memberikan manfaat besar bagi pengelolaan keuangan perusahaan (treasury) dalam tiga tahun mendatang.

Angka ini jauh di atas rata-rata Asia Pasifik sebesar 72%, menurut survei HSBC bertajuk Redefining Treasury in Asia Pacific: Voices of Treasury 2026.

Optimisme tersebut juga tercermin dari implementasi AI di perusahaan. Survei HSBC menunjukkan, sebanyak 34% manajer keuangan korporasi di Indonesia telah menggunakan AI agent untuk mengotomatisasi proses transaksi treasury. Angka ini hampir dua kali lipat rata-rata Asia Pasifik yang sebesar 17%.

Baca Juga: Bank Neo Commerce Tunjuk Christian Lesmana Jadi Dirut Gantikan Eri Budiono

Adapun perusahaan di Indonesia yang belum mengadopsi AI untuk fungsi treasury hanya sebesar 7%, lebih rendah dibandingkan rata-rata kawasan Asia Pasifik sebesar 19%.

Head of Global Payments Solutions Asia ex Greater China HSBC Manoj Dugar mengatakan, pembahasan mengenai AI kini telah bergeser dari sekadar eksplorasi menuju penerapan nyata di perusahaan.

“Pembicaraan mengenai AI telah bergeser dari sekadar rasa ingin tahu menjadi kapabilitas. Pembeda utamanya kini terletak pada implementasi, yaitu membenahi fondasi seperti data, tata kelola, dan kontrol, sehingga AI dapat diterapkan dalam skala lebih besar dan memberikan dampak dengan cepat,” ujar Manoj di Jakarta, Kamis (29/10).

Penerapan teknologi tersebut menjadi semakin penting di tengah volatilitas nilai tukar dan suku bunga. Pengelolaan transaksi keuangan di tengah kondisi pasar tersebut menjadi salah satu prioritas utama korporasi dalam 12 bulan mendatang, baik di Indonesia maupun di kawasan Asia Pasifik.

Meski optimistis terhadap manfaat AI, korporasi di Indonesia masih menghadapi tantangan dalam memperluas implementasinya. Sebanyak 80% responden menyebut integrasi dengan sistem yang sudah ada sebagai hambatan utama adopsi AI, lebih tinggi dibandingkan rata-rata Asia Pasifik sebesar 70%.

Hambatan lainnya meliputi keterbatasan tenaga ahli dan risiko, yang masing-masing disebut oleh 40% responden. Selain itu, 33% responden menyoroti biaya implementasi, sedangkan 27% menyebut kualitas data sebagai tantangan.

Dalam mewujudkan pengelolaan keuangan secara real time, sebanyak 48% manajer keuangan di Indonesia juga menilai persetujuan anggaran untuk implementasi sistem menjadi salah satu tantangan terbesar. Sementara itu, risiko keamanan dan upaya meyakinkan pemangku kepentingan mengenai manfaat perubahan masing-masing menjadi perhatian 37% responden.

Baca Juga: Dua Direktur JTrust Bank Borong Saham BCIC, Beli di Harga Rp 110 per Saham

Country Head of Global Payments Solutions HSBC Indonesia Anne Suhandojo mengatakan, perusahaan di Indonesia kini lebih berfokus pada percepatan adopsi AI dibandingkan sekadar mempertanyakan urgensi teknologi tersebut.

“Perusahaan di Indonesia tidak lagi bertanya apakah AI penting, melainkan fokus pada akselerasi adopsi AI. Bank akan semakin berperan sebagai mitra strategis dalam ekosistem manajemen keuangan perusahaan yang cerdas (intelligent treasury management), tidak lagi hanya menjadi penyedia layanan perbankan,” ujar Anne.

Seiring meningkatnya kebutuhan transaksi secara real time, keamanan siber juga menjadi perhatian dalam pengelolaan treasury. Survei HSBC menunjukkan, pembayaran (payment) dinilai sebagai transaksi treasury yang paling terpapar risiko siber, baik di Indonesia maupun di 10 dari 11 pasar Asia Pasifik yang disurvei.

Untuk mengantisipasi risiko tersebut, sebanyak 77% responden di Indonesia menilai perusahaan perlu meningkatkan investasi teknologi guna memperkuat keamanan transaksi. Proporsi ini jauh di atas rata-rata Asia Pasifik sebesar 57% dan menjadi yang tertinggi ketiga di kawasan.

Temuan tersebut menunjukkan bahwa meskipun korporasi Indonesia relatif optimistis terhadap pemanfaatan AI, keberhasilan implementasinya tetap bergantung pada kesiapan sistem, kualitas data, tata kelola, dan penguatan keamanan transaksi.

#180. Republican backers dominate list of top 10 midterm donors as Elon Musk boosts head-spinning 2026 total

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Republican megadonors are motivated by crypto and AI this midterm cycle. Among the top 10 donors for the 2026 midterm elections, eight are pushing for Republican control of Congress. 

The top 40 donors gave over $2.4 billion by mid-October 2026, compared with a much smaller inflation-adjusted total of $307.8 million in 2014, the Brennan Center for Justice found. That year, the top donors favored Democrats.

Elon Musk, who Brennan reports did not previously appear in the top 40 midterm donors, broke the record this October for the largest individual political donor in U.S. history.

The Tesla and SpaceX CEO distributed $446 million of his money toward America PAC, the Senate Leadership Fund and the Congressional Leadership Fund, according to a report by the Washington Post. 

Throw me the money

Other standout donors from the top 10 include shipping magnates Elizabeth and Richard Uihlein, who donated $150 million, and OpenAI co-founder Greg Brockman and his wife, Anna, who contributed $50 million to MAGA Inc. and an AI-related super PAC. 

Investor and philanthropist George Soros took the No. 4 spot by giving $102 million to a liberal super PAC, and former mayor of New York Mike Bloomberg was at No. 6, pitching in $84.2 million to Democratic campaigns. 

Donors this cycle have come from different industries than previous donors, highlighting a focus on crypto and AI, the Brennan Center for Justice found. Crypto makes up 13% of the top 40 donors, along with the finance industry.

Leading up to the 2022 midterm elections, the top 10 donors contributed a total of $505 million, WaPo reports.

Correction: Total giving by top 40 donors in 2014 through mid-October was $307.8 million, not $197.5 million as an earlier version of this story stated. The story has also been updated to reflect the true 2026 total spending figure, which was adjusted by the Brennan Center due to a miscount.

#350. Menperin Minta Data Center Dikembangkan di Klaster Khusus, Ini Alasannya

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Diki Mardiansyah | Editor: Noverius Laoli

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. Pemerintah mendorong pengembangan pusat data atau data center ditempatkan di kawasan khusus atau klaster tertentu.

Langkah ini dinilai penting mengingat operasional data center membutuhkan dukungan listrik dan air dalam jumlah besar.

Menteri Perindustrian Muhammad Sarmuji mengatakan, pengembangan data center perlu memperhatikan ketersediaan infrastruktur pendukung, terutama listrik dan air.

Karena itu, pembangunan data center sebaiknya tidak bercampur dengan kawasan permukiman.

Baca Juga: Investor Data Center Berskala Besar Harus Pasok Energi Sendiri, IDPRO Buka Suara

"Itu harus ditempatkan di tempat yang agak khusus. Kalau bisa data center di klaster, karena membutuhkan daya dukung yang besar, listrik, air terutama," ujar Sarmuji di Kantor Kemenperin, Kamis (29/10/2026).

Menurut dia, kebutuhan air yang besar untuk operasional data center perlu dikelola agar tidak menimbulkan persaingan dengan kebutuhan masyarakat di sekitar kawasan.

"Kita jangan sampai data center itu rebutan air dengan kawasan perumahan atau pemukiman, itu nanti akan jadi konflik," katanya.

Sarmuji menyebut, salah satu opsi yang dapat dipertimbangkan adalah menempatkan data center di lokasi yang berdekatan dengan pantai. Dengan begitu, kebutuhan air dapat dipenuhi melalui proses desalinasi air laut.

"Jadi perlu satu tempat khusus, mungkin lebih dekat dengan pantai supaya airnya berasal dari desalinasi air laut dan lain sebagainya, harus dipikirkan sampai ke sana," ujarnya.

Baca Juga: Kawasan Barelang Dibidik Jadi Kawasan Data Center, Kesiapan Infrastruktur Jadi Kunci

Sementara itu, Ketua Umum Himpunan Kawasan Industri (HKI) Akhmad Ma'ruf Maulana berharap data center dapat masuk dan dikembangkan di dalam kawasan industri.

Menurut dia, penempatan data center di kawasan industri akan memudahkan pengawasan sekaligus penyediaan infrastruktur pendukung, termasuk kebutuhan air dan listrik.

"Kami harapannya data center masuk ke dalam kawasan industri supaya mudah pengontrolannya juga, dan juga masalah air dan listrik juga kan di dalam," kata Akhmad.

Pengembangan data center di Indonesia terus meningkat seiring pertumbuhan ekonomi digital dan kebutuhan infrastruktur komputasi.

Baca Juga: Data Center Jatiluhur Ditunda, WALHI Minta Kebutuhan Air Dikaji

Namun, kebutuhan energi dan air menjadi salah satu aspek yang perlu diperhitungkan dalam pengembangan fasilitas tersebut agar tidak menimbulkan tekanan terhadap infrastruktur dan masyarakat sekitar.

#243. Some investors have midterm election jitters. Here’s what history shows about stocks

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With the midterm elections approaching, some investors worry about how the results will impact their portfolios. Whether their anxiety is warranted is another question.

Nearly half of surveyed investors — 48% — said they are concerned about the Nov. 3 midterms' potential impact on the stock market, according to Morgan Stanley Wealth Management's latest quarterly retail investor pulse survey, conducted Oct. 1-23 among 953 U.S. investors.

Separately, the Nationwide Retirement Institute, a research and education division of insurer Nationwide, found that 56% of surveyed investors said they feel more anxious because of the elections, and 75% believe the results could meaningfully affect their long-term financial plans. That survey was conducted Aug. 24-Sept. 11 among 2,029 investors with investable assets of at least $10,000.

"This summer and fall, the midterms have been probably the first topic of conversation with almost every [client] meeting that I've had," said John Mullen, managing director, president and CEO of Parsons Capital Management in Providence, Rhode Island. The firm ranked No. 25 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 list for 2026.

Read coverage of CNBC's 2026 Financial Advisor 100 list, including stories featuring ranked firms and their advisors:

While it's impossible to predict how the markets will behave following the midterms, historical data offers some perspective on how stocks have performed around past midterm elections.

Voters will be heading to the polls amid broad concerns about affordability and dissatisfaction with President Donald Trump's handling of the economy. The buildout of artificial intelligence through data centers, as well as the Iran war, also weigh heavily on voters' minds at a time when Republicans control both chambers of Congress.

Midterms historically have been unfavorable for the president's party, which has gained House seats in only three such elections since 1906, according to an analysis by Michael Townsend, Charles Schwab's managing director of legislative and regulatory affairs.

Based on polls, Democrats are favored to take control of the House — they lead Republicans on a generic ballot by about 9 percentage points as of Oct. 28, according to FiftyPlusOne, a website that tracks poll results. Democrats are also narrowly favored to win the Senate, according to DecisionDeskHQ, an election forecaster. The new Congress is scheduled to convene Jan. 3.

If Democrats take control of at least one chamber of Congress, the result will be a divided government instead of the unified government — the House, Senate and presidency all of the same party — that's been in place during Trump's second term.

An analysis of the S&P 500 index's performance over the past 50 years by asset manager Janus Henderson shows heightened market volatility when power in Washington is split between Republicans and Democrats, but little difference in the annualized price returns under a unified or divided government — 8.73% and 9.24%, respectively.

"Higher volatility during years with split control may have been caused by policy uncertainty, but a counterargument is that split government potentially insulated voters — and the economy — from some of the most ambitious intentions of a single party," wrote Seth Meyer, global head of client portfolio management for Janus Henderson. "In short, gridlock may be good."

Meanwhile, stock market volatility has tended to run higher in midterm election years, particularly in the months leading up to the election, according to a Capital Group analysis. Since 1970, median annualized volatility — a measure of market fluctuations — during midterm years has been nearly 16% vs. 13% in other years.

Nevertheless, "after the midterms, in almost every case, we've seen a rebound as the market settles into the known and [investors] kind of get the jitters out about what they didn't know before the election happened," said Michael Gibb, president and CEO of Godsey & Gibb Wealth Management in Richmond, Virginia. The firm ranked No. 89 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 list for 2026.

In midterm election years since 1974, the S&P 500 has averaged a 5.7% return in the three months right after a midterm election, with positive returns in 11 of the 13 years analyzed, according to the analysis by Schwab's Townsend. Over the six months following the midterms, the index has averaged a 12.4% return, with positive returns in all 13 years.

Separately, in 2024, Fidelity Investments analyzed S&P 500 returns from 1950 through 2023, dividing the presidential cycle into four successive 12-month periods running from Nov. 30 to Nov. 30. Average returns were 8.3% in the first year following a presidential election; 3.4% in year two, which is when the midterms are; 14.7% in year three, right after the midterms; and 9.1% in year four, the next presidential election year. The figures include reinvested dividends.

As for how the economy has performed after a midterm election, it's been mixed. Across 19 midterm cycles from 1950 through 2022, economic growth accelerated in nine cycles, slowed in nine and remained the same in one, according to an Oct. 26 research note by Sung Won Sohn, professor of finance and economics at Loyola Marymount University and chief economist at SS Economics.

And inflation? Another mixed bag, according to Sohn: It declined in 11 of the 19 post-midterm years and increased in eight. Yet average inflation remained relatively the same, slipping from about 3.63% in midterm years to 3.56% the following year.

Nevertheless, some investors may want to shift their holdings by rebalancing their portfolios. After the S&P posted an 18.11% loss in 2022, including reinvested dividends, it had a total return of 88.11% from the start of 2023 through the end of 2025. So far this year, its total return is 14.17% through Oct. 28.

These gains mean some investors whose portfolios started the period with, say, an intentional 80% stock and 20% bond allocation may now have more stocks than they intended.

"If there are investors who are overexposed to stocks because of the multiyear bull market we've been in, then it's not a bad idea to bring yourself back to the risk tolerance you set for yourself in a calculated and non-emotional manner," Gibb said. "But as for clients who are where their allocation should be over the long term, we wouldn't recommend making a change."

Likewise, investors who are nervous and may not be able to stomach volatility may want to reassess their portfolio's risk level to ensure it aligns with their comfort level and long-term financial goals.

"If you're not comfortable with what your asset mix is, if it's too risky for your own level of risk tolerance, then you have to make a change," Mullen said.

"We always tell our clients there's no magic number for percentages in stocks, bonds, cash and alternatives," he said. "It's whatever you're comfortable with, so you don't let your emotions get the best of you and go all to cash when the market's falling."

Additionally, making big moves can upend your long-term investment strategy.

"If you make too many changes and get away from what's worked for you, you run the risk of being disappointed if things don't turn out the way that you're expecting," Mullen said.

#41. US interest rates held steady and economy grows - what it means for you

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The US Federal Reserve has held interest rates steady at 3.75% - 4%.

The decision comes within a week of the midterm elections and after the central bank raised interest rates for the first time in three years at its September meeting.

"The overwhelming consensus, the overwhelming judgement, is that what we've done in this monetary medicine is starting to work some," Fed Chair Kevin Warsh said, referring to September's interest rate rise.

Figures published after the Fed's announcement showed the US economy growing at a stronger pace than expected, with inflation running above the Fed's target.

An interest rate tells people and businesses how much it costs to borrow money â such as with credit cards, mortgages, or business loans â but also what return they get for saving cash.

The Fed influences interest rates in the US through its target range for the federal funds rate, which is the interest rate used by commercial banks for overnight borrowing.

In its decision to hold rates steady this month, the Fed said that while inflation remains high, it wanted to gather more information before making another decision to change rates.

The Fed also noted that while there was uncertainty, the US economy remained resilient.

Warsh acknowledged that inflation remained too high, but noted that there were other signs - such as tightening credit conditions - that the economy was moving in the right direction.

There is plenty more economic data to come between now and the committee's next meeting, the Fed chair added, which should paint a more detailed picture of the state of the economy.

At that point, Warsh said, "we'll be, I think, better positioned to determine the right policy decision and the stance of policy."

While last month the committee signalled there could be one more rate rise this year, Warsh would not be drawn out on where he thinks they will go in six weeks' time.

"A lot of things can happen, so I'm not going to be able to share with you my musings."

The decision to hold rates steady was not unanimous.

While 10 of the 12 committee members voted to keep rates unchanged, Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack voted to increase rates to 4%-4.25%, from its current 3.75%-4%.

The bank's next meeting will be held over 8-9 December.

Warsh said things are moving in the right direction, while the US economy is performing very strongly.

The US economy grew by 3.4% on an annualised basis in the three months to September, stronger than analysts had expected.

That strong growth was driven by an acceleration in investment, and strong consumer spending.

While there was an annualised increase in personal spending of 3.8%, the annualised increase in private investment was a huge 19.4% which analysts say was largely due to investment in artificial intelligence.

Inflation is also above the Fed's target range.

Prices increased by 3.7% in the year to September, driven higher by fuel and energy prices which have surged off the back of the war in Iran.

The Fed uses interest rate changes to try to keep inflation â the rate at which prices change â at 2% a year and keep the job market stable.

When inflation is too high, the Fed tends to raise interest rates.

This tends to mean higher rates from banks and other lenders, which in turn affects spending and saving decisions.

For example, someone refinancing their mortgage or buying a property for the first time could face higher mortgage rates. It could also mean higher interest rates on credit cards or car loans. However, it means higher interest rates on savings as well.

This encourages people and businesses to save more, spend less money, and make less investments. It also encourages companies to hire less people or slow the pace of pay rises.

All of this tends to reduce demand and slow inflation.

When inflation is low or if the Fed believes the economy needs a boost, it cut rates.

The idea is that this will have the opposite affect, with banks dropping rates leading to more spending and less saving from people and businesses.

Leaving the rates unchanged means rates on things like new mortgages or business loans are also less likely to change. It also means interest rates on cash savings are unlikely to rise.

US interest rates have historically moved around quite a bit.

The last time Fed rates were as high as they have been recently was in the lead up to and during the start of the 2008 global financial crisis.

The Fed then dropped rates to historic lows to encourage spending and job creation during the economic slump and kept them at those lows for several years.

They picked up again ahead of the pandemic before dropping to as low as 0%-0.25% during the pandemic. They then rose to highs of 5.25%-5% from the middle of 2023 to August 2024 as inflation jumped.

They had been falling steadily since then, until rates were raised last month.

US inflation rises more than expected - here's what is happening with prices

#366. Pendapatan Prodia Diagnostic Line (PRDL) Anjlok 60,9% hingga September 2026

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reporter: Diki Mardiansyah | Editor: Ignatia Maria Sri Sayekti

KONTAN.CO.ID - JAKARTA. PT Prodia Diagnostic Line Tbk (PRDL) membukukan penurunan kinerja sepanjang sembilan bulan pertama 2026. Pendapatan perusahaan turun 60,9% secara tahunan menjadi Rp21,48 miliar dari Rp54,99 miliar pada periode yang sama tahun lalu.

Berdasarkan segmennya, Kimia Klinik masih menjadi kontributor terbesar dengan pendapatan Rp11,58 miliar. Namun, angka tersebut anjlok 74,4% dibandingkan 9M2025 yang mencapai Rp45,27 miliar.

"Penurunan pendapatan terutama dipengaruhi penyesuaian pola pembelian pelanggan serta perubahan kebutuhan pada sejumlah kategori produk," kata manajemen PRDL dalam keterangannya, Kamis (29/10/2026).

Penurunan juga terjadi pada segmen Hematologi yang turun 32,1% menjadi Rp2,03 miliar. Pendapatan Jasa merosot 64,6% menjadi Rp307 juta, sedangkan Imunologi dan Biomolekuler turun 48,9% menjadi Rp11 juta.

Baca Juga: Prodia Diagnostic Line (PRDL) Siapkan 17 Produk Baru dan Perluas Pasar

Di tengah penurunan tersebut, segmen Instrumen justru mencatat pertumbuhan. Pendapatan dari segmen ini naik 29,5% secara tahunan menjadi Rp7,56 miliar. Kontribusinya terhadap total pendapatan juga meningkat menjadi 35,2% dari sebelumnya 10,6%.

Penurunan pendapatan turut menekan profitabilitas PRDL. Laba kotor pada 9M2026 tercatat Rp6,58 miliar, turun 82% dari Rp36,65 miliar pada periode yang sama tahun lalu. Margin laba kotor pun menyusut menjadi 30,6% dari 66,7%.

Alhasil, PRDL membukukan rugi operasi Rp9,3 miliar dan rugi bersih Rp11,09 miliar pada 9M2026. Pada periode yang sama tahun lalu, perusahaan masih mencatat laba operasi Rp19,84 miliar dan laba bersih Rp13,71 miliar.

Meski demikian, tekanan terhadap EBITDA mulai mereda pada kuartal III-2026. LBITDA sepanjang 9M2026 tercatat Rp4,34 miliar, tetapi pada kuartal III hanya sebesar Rp43 juta. Beban usaha pada kuartal tersebut juga turun 43% secara tahunan menjadi Rp3,3 miliar.

Baca Juga: Prodia Widyahusada (PRDA) Targetkan Pendapatan Tumbuh hingga 5% pada 2026

Dari sisi neraca, kondisi PRDL justru semakin kuat. Kas dan setara kas per September 2026 mencapai Rp21,8 miliar, naik dari Rp10,8 miliar pada akhir 2025.

Perusahaan juga telah membayar utang bank sebesar Rp35,8 miliar sepanjang 9M2026. Alhasil, total utang berbunga turun 35,7% menjadi Rp60,6 miliar dari Rp94,3 miliar pada akhir 2025. Rasio utang berbunga terhadap ekuitas juga turun menjadi 0,46 kali dari 1,14 kali.

Ke depan, PRDL akan mengejar pertumbuhan melalui perluasan basis pelanggan, khususnya rumah sakit dan laboratorium swasta. Perusahaan juga memperkuat sinergi dengan PT Prodia Widyahusada Tbk (PRDA), mengikuti tender serta mengembangkan produk baru dan solusi point of care testing (POCT).

Selain itu, PRDL melihat peluang pertumbuhan dari layanan kalibrasi dan pemeliharaan instrumen. Perusahaan juga akan mengoptimalkan fasilitas produksi baru yang memiliki kapasitas hingga empat kali fasilitas sebelumnya untuk meningkatkan utilisasi dan penyerapan biaya tetap.

#176. Microsoft Unveils Local AI Coding Agents and Sandboxed Environments for Developers

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft has just announced an exciting evolution in software development tools, specifically designed to enhance the efficiency of small business owners and developers. With the introduction of local models like the MAI Code 1.1 Flash and new capabilities in GitHub Copilot, Microsoft aims to empower small businesses with enhanced coding and automation functionalities that prioritize both choice and control.

As businesses increasingly rely on technology for their operations, the ability to effectively manage coding tasks has never been more critical. Small business owners often juggle multiple priorities, and streamlined tools can significantly enhance productivity.

GitHub Copilot is now set to automatically determine whether tasks should use local on-device intelligence or cloud-scale models. This seamless orchestration occurs behind the scenes, enabling developers to focus on their core tasks without the stress of managing complex infrastructure. “Rather than forcing developers to manage infrastructure decisions themselves, GitHub Copilot automatically coordinates local and cloud inference,” Microsoft explains in their recent press release.

For businesses running on NVIDIA RTX Spark Windows PCs like the Surface Laptop Ultra, this means enhanced performance right at the edge. Local operations can now benefit from powerful inference models that deliver a remarkable coding experience. Combined with Microsoft Execution Containers (MXC), which support secure agentic coding sessions, small business developers can automate tasks while maintaining security and confidence in their systems.

Small business owners can now leverage these new advancements in several practical ways. For instance, using GitHub Copilot’s intelligent Auto orchestration, developers can allow the platform to decide the best inference model for different coding tasks, optimizing for performance and cost. Alternatively, for businesses that require specific models or direct control over certain workflows, the ability to explicitly select a local model provides flexible options.

Innovative features such as dynamic memory management in local inference help reduce overhead, ensuring that even those with limited technical resources can effectively harness AI to enhance their coding capabilities. The focus has been placed on preserving the integrity and quality of the output, as small mistakes in code can lead to significant complications, especially for businesses that rely heavily on software solutions.

However, while the new tools present numerous advantages, small business owners should consider potential challenges. The technological requirements for leveraging local inference models include having the specific hardware capacity, such as the aforementioned Surface Laptop Ultra. While these machines are designed for performance, they come at a cost that might not fit every small business’s budget.

Moreover, there is a learning curve attached to adopting the latest AI-driven tools. Businesses may need to invest time and resources into training their teams to fully understand and utilize new functionalities effectively.

The sandboxing features, powered by Microsoft Execution Containers, introduce another layer of complexity. While sandboxing provides enhanced security for local coding by controlling access to files and network resources, it may require additional management to ensure that operations run smoothly without unintended disruptions to standard workflows.

Microsoft is optimistic about the benefits these upgrades can bring to the coding community. “The result is poised to be the next step in the HydraFusion vision: intelligent orchestration that spans not just multiple models but multiple compute environments including the edge,” they state. As businesses experiment with automation and more cohesive coding tools, it can lead to significant gains in efficiency, allowing them to compete more effectively in a tech-driven market.

In practice, an example of utilizing these new capabilities could involve setting up automated daily reports for local repositories. By combining the tasks of reading repositories, running tests, and generating HTML reports into a daily automation script, small business owners can save considerable time and reduce manual errors.

Microsoft’s push to integrate local models and sandbox tools marks a noteworthy advancement for small businesses looking to harness the power of AI in development while maintaining control over their coding environments. As these tools roll out, businesses can anticipate impactful changes that streamline their development processes and enhance productivity.

For those interested in diving deeper into these advancements, additional details can be found at the original post on Microsoft’s Command Line Blog.
